Balancing</h1></header>
<p>LiteLLM manages:</p>
<ul>
<li>Load-balance across multiple deployments (e.g. Azure/OpenAI)</li>
<li>Prioritizing important requests to ensure they don't fail (i.e. Queueing)</li>
<li>Basic reliability logic - cooldowns, fallbacks, timeouts and retries (fixed + exponential backoff) across multiple deployments/providers.</li>
</ul>
<p>In production, litellm supports using Redis as a way to track cooldown server and usage (managing tpm/rpm limits).</p>
<div class="theme-admonition theme-admonition-info admonition_xJq3 alert alert--info"><div class=admonitionHeading_Gvgb><span class=admonitionIcon_Rf37><svg viewBox="0 0 14 16"><path fill-rule=evenodd d="M7 2.3c3.14 0 5.7 2.56 5.7 5.7s-2.56 5.7-5.7 5.7A5.71 5.71 0 0 1 1.3 8c0-3.14 2.56-5.7 5.7-5.7zM7 1C3.14 1 0 4.14 0 8s3.14 7 7 7 7-3.14 7-7-3.14-7-7-7zm1 3H6v5h2V4zm0 6H6v2h2v-2z"/></svg></span>info</div><div class=admonitionContent_BuS1><p>If you want a server to load balance across different LLM APIs, use our <a href=/docs/proxy/load_balancing>LiteLLM Proxy Server</a></div></div>
<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=load-balancing>Load Balancing<a href=#load-balancing class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Load Balancing" title="Direct link to Load Balancing">​</a></h2>
<p>(s/o <a href=https://www.linkedin.com/in/paulpierre/ target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer">@paulpierre</a> and <a href=https://docs.sweep.dev/blogs/openai-proxy target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer">sweep proxy</a> for their contributions to this implementation)
<a href=https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/main/litellm/router.py target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>See Code</strong></a></p>
<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=quick-start>Quick Start<a href=#quick-start class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Quick Start" title="Direct link to Quick Start">​</a></h3>
<p>Loadbalance across multiple <a href=/docs/providers/azure>azure</a>/<a href=/docs/providers/bedrock>bedrock</a>/<a href=/docs/providers/>provider</a> deployments. LiteLLM will handle retrying in different regions if a call fails.</p>

<li>Start proxy</li>
</ol><div class="language-bash codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div class=codeBlockContent_QJqH><pre tabindex=0 class="prism-code language-bash codeBlock_bY9V thin-scrollbar" style=color:#000000;background-color:#ffffff><code class=codeBlockLines_e6Vv><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">litellm --config /path/to/config.yaml </span><br></span></code></pre></div></div><ol start=3>
<li>Test it!</li>
</ol><div class="language-bash codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div class=codeBlockContent_QJqH><pre tabindex=0 class="prism-code language-bash codeBlock_bY9V thin-scrollbar" style=color:#000000;background-color:#ffffff><code class=codeBlockLines_e6Vv><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">curl -X POST 'http://0.0.0.0:4000/chat/completions' \</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">-H 'Content-Type: application/json' \</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">-H 'Authorization: Bearer sk-1234' \</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">-d '{</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">  "model": "gpt-3.5-turbo",</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">  "messages": [</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">        {"role": "user", "content": "Hi there!"}</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">    ],</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">    "mock_testing_rate_limit_error": true</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">}'</span><br></span></code></pre></div></div></div></div></div>
<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=available-endpoints>Available Endpoints<a href=#available-endpoints class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Available Endpoints" title="Direct link to Available Endpoints">​</a></h3>
<ul>
<li><code>router.completion()</code> - chat completions endpoint to call 100+ LLMs</li>
<li><code>router.acompletion()</code> - async chat completion calls</li>
<li><code>router.embedding()</code> - embedding endpoint for Azure, OpenAI, Huggingface endpoints</li>
<li><code>router.aembedding()</code> - async embeddings calls</li>
<li><code>router.text_completion()</code> - completion calls in the old OpenAI <code>/v1/completions</code> endpoint format</li>
<li><code>router.atext_completion()</code> - async text completion calls</li>
<li><code>router.image_generation()</code> - completion calls in OpenAI <code>/v1/images/generations</code> endpoint format</li>
<li><code>router.aimage_generation()</code> - async image generation calls</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=advanced---routing-strategies-️>Advanced - Routing Strategies ⭐️<a href=#advanced---routing-strategies-️ class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Advanced - Routing Strategies ⭐️" title="Direct link to Advanced - Routing Strategies ⭐️">​</a></h2>
<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=routing-strategies---weighted-pick-rate-limit-aware-least-busy-latency-based-cost-based>Routing Strategies - Weighted Pick, Rate Limit Aware, Least Busy, Latency Based, Cost Based<a href=#routing-strategies---weighted-pick-rate-limit-aware-least-busy-latency-based-cost-based class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Routing Strategies - Weighted Pick, Rate Limit Aware, Least Busy, Latency Based, Cost Based" title="Direct link to Routing Strategies - Weighted Pick, Rate Limit Aware, Least Busy, Latency Based, Cost Based">​</a></h4>
<p>Router provides multiple strategies for routing your calls across multiple deployments. <strong>We recommend using <code>simple-shuffle</code> (default) for best performance in production.</strong></p>
<div class="tabs-container tabList__CuJ"><ul role=tablist aria-orientation=horizontal class=tabs><li role=tab tabindex=0 aria-selected=true class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP tabs__item--active">(Default) Weighted Pick - RECOMMENDED<li role=tab tabindex=-1 aria-selected=false class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP">Rate-Limit Aware v2 (ASYNC)<li role=tab tabindex=-1 aria-selected=false class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP">Latency-Based<li role=tab tabindex=-1 aria-selected=false class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP">Rate-Limit Aware<li role=tab tabindex=-1 aria-selected=false class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP">Least-Busy<li role=tab tabindex=-1 aria-selected=false class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP">Custom Routing Strategy<li role=tab tabindex=-1 aria-selected=false class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP">Lowest Cost Routing (Async)</ul><div class=margin-top--md><div role=tabpanel class=tabItem_Ymn6><p><strong>Default and Recommended for Production</strong> - Best performance with minimal latency overhead.<p>Picks a deployment based on the provided <strong>Requests per minute (rpm) or Tokens per minute (tpm)</strong><p>If <code>rpm</code> or <code>tpm</code> is not provided, it randomly picks a deployment<p>You can also set a <code>weight</code> param, to specify which model should get picked when.<div class="tabs-container tabList__CuJ"><ul role=tablist aria-orientation=horizontal class=tabs><li role=tab tabindex=0 aria-selected=true class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP tabs__item--active">RPM-based shuffling<li role=tab tabindex=-1 aria-selected=false class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP">Weight-based shuffling</ul><div class=margin-top--md><div role=tabpanel class=tabItem_Ymn6><h5 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" <p>[!WARNING]<br>
<strong>Usage-based routing is not recommended for production due to performance impacts.</strong> Use <code>simple-shuffle</code> (default) for optimal performance in high-traffic scenarios. Usage-based routing adds significant latency due to Redis operations for tracking usage across deployments.</p>
</blockquote><p><strong>🎉 NEW</strong> This is an async implementation of usage-based-routing.<p><strong>Filters out deployment if tpm/rpm limit exceeded</strong> - If you pass in the deployment's tpm/rpm limits.<p>Routes to <strong>deployment with lowest TPM usage</strong> for that minute.<p>In production, we use Redis to track usage (TPM/RPM) across multiple deployments. This implementation uses <strong>async redis calls</strong> (redis.incr and redis.mget).<p>For Azure, <a href=https://stackoverflow.com/questions/77368844/what-is-the-request-per-minute-rate-limit-for-azure-openai-models-for-gpt-3-5-tu target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer">you get 6 RPM per 1000 TPM</a><div class="tabs-container tabList__CuJ"><ul role=tablist aria-orientation=horizontal class=tabs><li role=tab tabindex=0 aria-selected=true class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP id=set-lowest-latency-buffer>Set Lowest Latency Buffer<a href=#set-lowest-latency-buffer class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Set Lowest Latency Buffer" title="Direct link to Set Lowest Latency Buffer">​</a></h4><p>Set a buffer within which deployments are candidates for making calls to.<p>E.g.<p>if you have 5 deployments<div class="language-text codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div class=codeBlockContent_QJqH><pre tabindex=0 class="prism-code language-text codeBlock_bY9V thin-scrollbar" style=color:#000000;background-color:#ffffff><code class=codeBlockLines_e6Vv><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">https://litellm-prod-1.openai.azure.com/: 0.07s</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">https://litellm-prod-2.openai.azure.com/: 0.1s</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">https://litellm-prod-3.openai.azure.com/: 0.1s</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">https://litellm-prod-4.openai.azure.com/: 0.1s</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">https://litellm-prod-5.openai.azure.com/: 4.66s</span><br></span></code></pre></div></div><p>to prevent initially overloading <code>prod-1</code>, with all requests - we can set a buffer of 50%, to consider deployments <code>prod-2, prod-3, prod-4</code>.<p><strong>In Router</strong><div class="language-python codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div hidden><p>This will route to the deployment with the lowest TPM usage for that minute.<p>In production, we use Redis to track usage (TPM/RPM) across multiple deployments.<p>If you pass in the deployment's tpm/rpm limits, this will also check against that, and filter out any who's limits would be exceeded.<p>For Azure, your RPM = TPM/6.<div class="language-python codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 165)">)</span><br></span></code></pre></div></div></div><div role=tabpanel class=tabItem_Ymn6 hidden><p>Picks a deployment based on the lowest cost<p>How this works:<ul>
<li>Get all healthy deployments</li>
<li>Select all deployments that are under their provided <code>rpm/tpm</code> limits</li>
<li>For each deployment check if <code>litellm_param["model"]</code> exists in <a href=https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/main/model_prices_and_context_window.json target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer"><code>litellm_model_cost_map</code></a>
<ul>
<li>if deployment does not exist in <code>litellm_model_cost_map</code> -> use deployment_cost= <code>$1</code></li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>Select deployment with lowest cost</li>
</ul><div class="language-python codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div class=codeBlockContent_QJqH><pre tabindex=0 <h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=routing-groups---per-model-strategies-and-callable-virtual-models>Routing Groups - Per-Model Strategies and Callable Virtual Models<a href=#routing-groups---per-model-strategies-and-callable-virtual-models class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Routing Groups - Per-Model Strategies and Callable Virtual Models" title="Direct link to Routing Groups - Per-Model Strategies and Callable Virtual Models">​</a></h2>
<p>Apply different routing strategies to different models in the same router. A <strong>routing group</strong> binds a list of <code>model_name</code>s to a strategy and (optionally) strategy args. Models not claimed by any group fall back to the router's top-level <code>routing_strategy</code>.</p>
<p>A group is also <strong>callable as a model</strong>: request <code>model: &lt;group_name></code> and LiteLLM picks among the union of every member's deployments using the group's strategy. Group names appear in <code>/v1/models</code>, so clients that discover models from the gateway (Claude Code with <code>CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_GATEWAY_MODEL_DISCOVERY=1</code>, Codex) surface them in their pickers.</p>
<div class="language-bash codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div class=codeBlockContent_QJqH><pre tabindex=0 class="prism-code language-bash codeBlock_bY9V thin-scrollbar" style=color:#000000;background-color:#ffffff><code class=codeBlockLines_e6Vv><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">curl http://localhost:4000/v1/chat/completions \</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">  -H "Authorization: Bearer $LITELLM_KEY" \</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">  -d '{"model": "anthropic-latency", "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "ping"}]}'</span><br></span></code></pre></div></div>
<p>Access control treats a group as its own model name: grant <code>&lt;group_name></code> on a key or team to let it list and call the group. Membership is not expanded in either direction, so a key granted only the group cannot call members directly and a key granted a member cannot call the group. A group name must not collide with an existing <code>model_name</code> or <code>model_group_alias</code>; config load rejects it. Requests keep the group name as <code>model_group</code> in spend logs, with each row recording the member deployment that actually served it. Fallbacks and <code>model_group_retry_policy</code> are keyed by name, so give the group its own entries if you need them. Claude Desktop's model picker only accepts Anthropic-shaped names, so name groups like <code>claude-quality</code> if Desktop matters.</p>
<div class="theme-admonition theme-admonition-tip admonition_xJq3 alert alert--success"><div class=admonitionHeading_Gvgb><span class=admonitionIcon_Rf37><svg viewBox="0 0 12 16"><path fill-rule=evenodd d="M6.5 0C3.48 0 1 2.19 1 5c0 .92.55 2.25 1 3 1.34 2.25 1.78 2.78 2 4v1h5v-1c.22-1.22.66-1.75 2-4 .45-.75 1-2.08 1-3 0-2.81-2.48-5-5.5-5zm3.64 7.48c-.25.44-.47.8-.67 1.11-.86 1.41-1.25 2.06-1.45 3.23-.02.05-.02.11-.02.17H5c0-.06 0-.13-.02-.17-.2-1.17-.59-1.83-1.45-3.23-.2-.31-.42-.67-.67-1.11C2.44 6.78 2 5.65 2 5c0-2.2 2.02-4 4.5-4 1.22 0 2.36.42 3.22 1.19C10.55 2.94 11 3.94 11 5c0 .66-.44 1.78-.86 2.48zM4 14h5c-.23 1.14-1.3 2-2.5 2s-2.27-.86-2.5-2z"/></svg></span>tip</div><div class=admonitionContent_BuS1><p>You can also create, edit, and delete routing groups from the dashboard. See <a href=/docs/proxy/ui/routing_groups>Manage Routing Groups via UI</a>.</div></div>
<p><strong>When to use this:</strong> you want latency-based routing for <code>gpt-4o</code>, but plain weighted-pick for cheaper models, without spinning up a second router.</p>
<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=rules>Rules<a href=#rules class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Rules" title="Direct link to Rules">​</a></h4>
<ul>
<li>Each <code>model_name</code> may belong to <strong>at most one</strong> group. Overlap raises <code>ValueError</code> at init.</li>
<li>Models not in any group use the top-level <code>routing_strategy</code> / <code>routing_strategy_args</code> (an implicit <code>"default"</code> group). The name <code>"default"</code> is reserved.</li>
<li>Each group can override <code>routing_strategy_args</code> (e.g. latency window TTL, TPM ceilings).</li>
<li>The group is resolved per-request based on the post-pre-routing-hook <code>model</code> name.</li>
</ul>

<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=multiple-groups>Multiple groups<a href=#multiple-groups class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Multiple groups" title="Direct link to Multiple groups">​</a></h4>
<p>Two groups can use the same strategy with different args; each gets an independent state instance.</p>

<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=updating-at-runtime>Updating at runtime<a href=#updating-at-runtime class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Updating at runtime" title="Direct link to Updating at runtime">​</a></h4>
<p>Routing groups can be updated via <code>Router.update_settings(routing_groups=[...])</code> or the proxy's <code>/config/update</code> endpoint. Per-group state is rebuilt on update.</p>
<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=traffic-mirroring--silent-experiments>Traffic Mirroring / Silent Experiments<a href=#traffic-mirroring--silent-experiments class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Traffic Mirroring / Silent Experiments" title="Direct link to Traffic Mirroring / Silent Experiments">​</a></h2>
<p>Traffic mirroring allows you to "mimic" production traffic to a secondary (silent) model for evaluation purposes. The silent model's response is gathered in the background and does not affect the latency or result of the primary request.</p>
<p><a href=/docs/traffic_mirroring><strong>See detailed guide on A/B Testing - Traffic Mirroring here</strong></a></p>
<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=basic-reliability>Basic Reliability<a href=#basic-reliability class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Basic Reliability" title="Direct link to Basic Reliability">​</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=deployment-ordering-priority>Deployment Ordering (Priority)<a href=#deployment-ordering-priority class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Deployment Ordering (Priority)" title="Direct link to Deployment Ordering (Priority)">​</a></h3>
<p>Set <code>order</code> in <code>litellm_params</code> to prioritize deployments. Lower values = higher priority. When multiple deployments share the same <code>order</code>, the routing strategy picks among them.</p>
<p>When a request to an <code>order=1</code> deployment fails (connection error, 404, 429, etc.), the router automatically tries <code>order=2</code> deployments, then <code>order=3</code>, and so on. Each order level gets its own set of retries before escalating to the next. If all order levels are exhausted, the router falls through to any configured fallbacks.</p>

<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=weighted-deployments>Weighted Deployments<a href=#weighted-deployments class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Weighted Deployments" title="Direct link to Weighted Deployments">​</a></h3>
<p>Set <code>weight</code> on a deployment to pick one deployment more often than others.</p>
<p>This works across <strong>simple-shuffle</strong> routing strategy (this is the default, if no routing strategy is selected).</p>

<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=weighted-failover>Weighted Failover<a href=#weighted-failover class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Weighted Failover" title="Direct link to Weighted Failover">​</a></h3>
<p>By default, when a deployment in a model group fails, the router moves on to the next entry in <code>fallbacks</code> (a different model group). With <code>enable_weighted_failover</code>, the router first retries <strong>inside the same model group</strong> by re-picking a different deployment using the existing weights, and only escalates to cross-group fallbacks once every deployment in the group has been tried.</p>
<p>This is useful when you have multiple regional copies of the same model (e.g. Azure <code>eastus2</code> + <code>swedencentral</code>) and want a failed region to fail over to a healthy peer with the same <code>model_name</code>, instead of immediately switching to a different model.</p>
<p><strong>Behavior</strong></p>
<ul>
<li>Only active when <code>routing_strategy="simple-shuffle"</code> (the default).</li>
<li>On a retryable failure, the failing deployment ID is excluded and a new deployment is picked from the remaining peers in the same model group, respecting <code>weight</code> / <code>rpm</code> / <code>tpm</code>.</li>
<li>Exclusions accumulate across hops: each retry adds the previous failure to the exclusion set, so a deployment that just failed is never picked again in the same request chain.</li>
<li>Capped by <code>max_fallbacks</code> (default <code>5</code>).</li>
<li>Not triggered for <code>ContextWindowExceededError</code> or <code>ContentPolicyViolationError</code>, which keep their dedicated fallback paths.</li>
<li>Async-only: honored by <code>router.acompletion()</code> and other async entrypoints. The sync <code>router.completion()</code> path falls through to regular fallbacks.</li>
<li>Cooldowns still apply: a deployment that crosses <code>allowed_fails</code> is cooled down independently of weighted failover.</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Order vs. weight</strong></p>
<p>If the same group also uses <code>order</code>, the order filter runs <strong>before</strong> the weighted pick. So weighted failover re-picks only among the deployments in the current minimum-order tier. Promotion to the next order tier happens through the existing order-based fallback path.</p>
<p><strong>Config</strong></p>

<p><strong>Walkthrough</strong></p>
<p>With the config above and a request to <code>gpt-4.1-mini</code>:</p>
<ol>
<li><code>simple-shuffle</code> picks one of the two deployments using <code>weight</code>.</li>
<li>If the picked deployment raises a provider error (e.g. <code>RateLimitError</code>, <code>InternalServerError</code>), its deployment ID is added to <code>metadata._failover_excluded_ids</code>.</li>
<li>The router re-enters <code>simple-shuffle</code> with the failed deployment excluded and weights renormalized over what's left.</li>
<li>Steps 2–3 repeat until a deployment succeeds, every peer has been excluded, or <code>max_fallbacks</code> is reached.</li>
<li>Only after all peers are exhausted does the router fall through to any <code>fallbacks</code> configured for the group.</li>
</ol>
<p>See <a href=/docs/proxy/config_settings#router_settings---reference><code>enable_weighted_failover</code></a> in the router settings reference for the flag.</p>
<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=max-parallel-requests-async>Max Parallel Requests (ASYNC)<a href=#max-parallel-requests-async class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Max Parallel Requests (ASYNC)" title="Direct link to Max Parallel Requests (ASYNC)">​</a></h3>
<p>Used in semaphore for async requests on router. Limit the max concurrent calls made to a deployment. Useful in high-traffic scenarios.</p>
<p>If tpm/rpm is set, and no max parallel request limit given, we use the RPM or calculated RPM (tpm/1000/6) as the max parallel request limit.</p>

<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=cooldowns>Cooldowns<a href=#cooldowns class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Cooldowns" title="Direct link to Cooldowns">​</a></h3>

<li>allowed_fails: 3</li>
<li>cooldown_time: 5s (<code>DEFAULT_COOLDOWN_TIME_SECONDS</code> in constants.py)</li>
</ul><p><strong>Set Per Model</strong><p><code>allowed_fails</code> and <code>cooldown_time</code> can also be set on a single deployment instead of the whole router. A deployment-level value overrides the router-level one for that deployment only, so a flaky third-party endpoint can get a shorter fuse than the rest of your fleet without affecting them.<div default</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">    </span><span class="token key atrule">cooldown_time</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">:</span><span class="token plain"> </span><span class="token number" style="color:rgb(9, 134, 88)">0</span><span class="token plain"> </span><span class="token comment" style="color:rgb(0, 128, 0)"># disable cooldowns for this deployment</span><br></span></code></pre></div></div><p><code>allowed_fails</code> must be set under <code>model_info</code>, not <code>litellm_params</code>: unlike <code>model_info</code>, <code>litellm_params</code> is copied into the actual request sent to the LLM provider, so a router-only setting placed there would leak into that request. <code>cooldown_time</code> can be set under either location (<code>model_info</code> takes priority if both are set), matching its pre-existing behavior on the router's primary failure path.</div></div></div>

<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=how-cooldowns-work>How Cooldowns Work<a href=#how-cooldowns-work class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to How Cooldowns Work" title="Direct link to How Cooldowns Work">​</a></h3>
<p>Cooldowns apply to individual deployments, not entire model groups. The router isolates failures to specific deployments while keeping healthy alternatives available.</p>
<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=what-is-a-deployment>What is a deployment?<a href=#what-is-a-deployment class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to What is a deployment?" title="Direct link to What is a deployment?">​</a></h4>
<p>A deployment is a single entry in your <code>config.yaml</code> model list. Each deployment represents a unique configuration with its own <code>litellm_params</code>.</p>
<p>LiteLLM generates a unique <code>model_id</code> for each deployment by creating a deterministic hash of all the <code>litellm_params</code>. This allows the router to track and manage each deployment independently.</p>
<p><strong>Example: Multiple deployments for the same model</strong></p>

<p>Each deployment gets a unique <code>model_id</code> (e.g., <code>1234567890</code>, <code>9129922</code>, <code>4982929292</code>) that the router uses for tracking health and cooldown status.</p>
<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=when-are-deployments-cooled-down>When are deployments cooled down?<a href=#when-are-deployments-cooled-down class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to When are deployments cooled down?" title="Direct link to When are deployments cooled down?">​</a></h4>
<p>The router automatically cools down deployments based on the following conditions:</p>
<table><thead><tr><th>Condition<th>Trigger<th>Cooldown Duration<tbody><tr><td><strong>Rate Limiting (429)</strong><td>Immediate on 429 response<td>5 seconds (default)<tr><td><strong>High Failure Rate</strong><td>>50% failures in current minute<td>5 seconds (default)<tr><td><strong>Non-Retryable Errors</strong><td>401 (Auth), 404 (Not Found), 408 (Timeout)<td>5 seconds (default)</table>
<p>During cooldown, the specific deployment is temporarily removed from the available pool, while other healthy deployments continue serving requests.</p>
<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=cooldown-recovery>Cooldown Recovery<a href=#cooldown-recovery class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Cooldown Recovery" title="Direct link to Cooldown Recovery">​</a></h4>
<p>Deployments automatically recover from cooldown after the cooldown period expires. The router will:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Monitor cooldown timers</strong> for each deployment</li>
<li><strong>Automatically re-enable</strong> deployments when cooldown expires</li>
<li><strong>Gradually reintroduce</strong> cooled-down deployments to the rotation</li>
<li><strong>Reset failure counters</strong> once the deployment is healthy again</li>
</ol>
<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=real-world-example>Real-World Example<a href=#real-world-example class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Real-World Example" title="Direct link to Real-World Example">​</a></h4>

<p><strong>Failure Scenario:</strong></p>
<!-- -->
<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=retries>Retries<a href=#retries class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Retries" title="Direct link to Retries">​</a></h3>
<p>For both async + sync functions, we support retrying failed requests.</p>
<p>For RateLimitError we implement exponential backoffs</p>
<p>For generic errors, we retry immediately</p>
<p>Here's a quick look at how we can set <code>num_retries = 3</code>:</p>

<p>We also support setting minimum time to wait before retrying a failed request. This is via the <code>retry_after</code> param.</p>

<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=where-num_retries-can-be-set-and-which-one-wins>Where <code>num_retries</code> can be set, and which one wins<a href=#where-num_retries-can-be-set-and-which-one-wins class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to where-num_retries-can-be-set-and-which-one-wins" title="Direct link to where-num_retries-can-be-set-and-which-one-wins">​</a></h4>
<p><code>num_retries</code> can come from four places. They are ranked, highest first:</p>
<ol>
<li>the <code>x-litellm-num-retries</code> request header (proxy only)</li>
<li><code>num_retries</code> in the request body</li>
<li><code>num_retries</code> in a deployment's <code>litellm_params</code> in <code>model_list</code></li>
<li><code>num_retries</code> in <code>litellm_settings</code> (the router-wide default)</li>
</ol>
<p>So a caller can always raise or lower the retry count for one request, including setting it to <code>0</code> to
disable retries, no matter what the deployment or the global setting says. A deployment value applies
whenever the request carries none, and it overrides the global default.</p>
<p><code>num_retries</code> is not the same knob as <code>max_retries</code>. <code>num_retries</code> is LiteLLM's own retry loop, while
<code>max_retries</code> is the provider SDK's internal retry count. For a call that goes through the router,
LiteLLM owns retries and pins the provider client to <code>max_retries: 0</code>, so a <code>max_retries</code> in the
request body or in <code>litellm_params</code> has no effect on a proxy request. That is deliberate: it is what
stops a deployment <code>num_retries: N</code> from being applied twice and turning one request into
<code>(1 + N) ** 2</code> upstream calls. Use <code>num_retries</code> to control how many attempts a request gets.</p>
<h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=advanced-custom-retries-cooldowns-based-on-error-type>[Advanced]: Custom Retries, Cooldowns based on Error Type<a href=#advanced-custom-retries-cooldowns-based-on-error-type class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to [Advanced]: Custom Retries, Cooldowns based on Error Type" title="Direct link to [Advanced]: Custom Retries, Cooldowns based on Error Type">​</a></h3>
<ul>
<li>Use <code>RetryPolicy</code> if you want to set a <code>num_retries</code> based on the Exception received</li>
<li>Use <code>AllowedFailsPolicy</code> to set a custom number of <code>allowed_fails</code>/minute before cooling down a deployment</li>
</ul>
<p><a href=https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/ccda616f2f881375d4e8586c76fe4662909a7d22/litellm/types/router.py#L436 target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>See All Exception Types</strong></a></p>

<p><code>AllowedFailsPolicy</code> also supports <code>ServiceUnavailableErrorAllowedFails</code>, <code>BadGatewayErrorAllowedFails</code>, and <code>NotFoundErrorAllowedFails</code>.</p>
<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=per-deployment-allowed_fails_policy>Per-deployment allowed_fails_policy<a href=#per-deployment-allowed_fails_policy class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Per-deployment allowed_fails_policy" title="Direct link to Per-deployment allowed_fails_policy">​</a></h4>
<p><code>allowed_fails_policy</code> can be scoped to a single deployment by setting it under that deployment's <code>model_info</code> instead of <code>router_settings</code>. A deployment-level policy takes full precedence over the router-level one for that deployment, so a rate-limited third-party endpoint can cool down after its first <code>RateLimitError</code> while the rest of your fleet keeps the router-wide tolerance.</p>

atrule">model_list</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">:</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain"></span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">-</span><span class="token plain"> </span><span class="token key atrule">model_name</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">:</span><span class="token plain"> gpt</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">-</span><span class="token number" style="color:rgb(9, 134, class="token key atrule">model</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">:</span><span class="token plain"> openai/gpt</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">-</span><span class="token number" style="color:rgb(9, 134, 88)">4</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span os.environ/OPENAI_API_KEY</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">  </span><span class="token key atrule">model_info</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">:</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">    </span><span class="token key RateLimitError</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">      </span><span class="token key <h3 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=caching>Caching<a href=#caching class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Caching" title="Direct link to Caching">​</a></h3>
<p>In production, we recommend using a Redis cache. For quickly testing things locally, we also support simple in-memory caching.</p>
<p><strong>In-memory Cache</strong></p>
<div class="language-python codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div class=codeBlockContent_QJqH><pre tabindex=0 class="prism-code language-python codeBlock_bY9V thin-scrollbar" style=color:#000000;background-color:#ffffff><code class=codeBlockLines_e6Vv><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">router </span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token plain"> Router</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">(</span><span class="token plain">model_list</span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token plain">model_list</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">,</span><span class="token plain"> </span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">                cache_responses</span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token boolean">True</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">)</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain" style=display:inline-block></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain"></span><span class="token keyword" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 255)">print</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">(</span><span class="token plain">response</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">)</span><br></span></code></pre></div></div>

<p><strong>Pass in Redis URL, additional kwargs</strong></p>

<div class="theme-admonition theme-admonition-info admonition_xJq3 alert alert--info"><div class=admonitionHeading_Gvgb><span class=admonitionIcon_Rf37><svg viewBox="0 0 14 16"><path fill-rule=evenodd d="M7 2.3c3.14 0 5.7 2.56 5.7 5.7s-2.56 5.7-5.7 5.7A5.71 5.71 0 0 1 1.3 8c0-3.14 2.56-5.7 5.7-5.7zM7 1C3.14 1 0 4.14 0 8s3.14 7 7 7 7-3.14 7-7-3.14-7-7-7zm1 3H6v5h2V4zm0 6H6v2h2v-2z"/></svg></span>info</div><div class=admonitionContent_BuS1><p>When configuring Redis caching in router settings, use <code>cache_kwargs</code> to pass additional Redis parameters, especially for non-string values that may fail when set via <code>REDIS_*</code> environment variables.</div></div>
<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=pre-call-checks-context-window-eu-regions>Pre-Call Checks (Context Window, EU-Regions)<a href=#pre-call-checks-context-window-eu-regions class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Pre-Call Checks (Context Window, EU-Regions)" title="Direct link to Pre-Call Checks (Context Window, EU-Regions)">​</a></h2>
<p>Enable pre-call checks to filter out:</p>
<ol>
<li>deployments with context window limit &lt; messages for a call.</li>
<li>deployments outside of eu-region</li>
</ol>
<div class="tabs-container tabList__CuJ"><ul role=tablist aria-orientation=horizontal class=tabs><li role=tab tabindex=0 aria-selected=true class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP tabs__item--active">SDK<li role=tab tabindex=-1 aria-selected=false class="tabs__item tabItem_LNqP">Proxy</ul><div class=margin-top--md><div role=tabpanel class=tabItem_Ymn6><p><strong>1. Enable pre-call checks</strong><div class="language-python codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div class=codeBlockContent_QJqH><pre tabindex=0 class="prism-code language-python codeBlock_bY9V thin-scrollbar" style=color:#000000;background-color:#ffffff><code class=codeBlockLines_e6Vv><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token keyword" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 255)">from</span><span class="token plain"> litellm </span><span class="token keyword" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 255)">import</span><span class="token plain"> Router </span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain"></span><span class="token comment" style="color:rgb(0, 128, 0)"># ...</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">router </span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token plain"> Router</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">(</span><span class="token plain">model_list</span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token plain">model_list</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">,</span><span class="token plain"> enable_pre_call_checks</span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token boolean">True</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">)</span><span class="token plain"> </span><span class="token comment" style="color:rgb(0, 128, 0)"># 👈 Set to True</span><br></span></code></pre></div></div><p><strong>2. Set Model List</strong><p>For context window checks on azure deployments, set the base model. Pick the base model from <a href=https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/main/model_prices_and_context_window.json target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer">this list</a>, all the azure models start with <code>azure/</code>.<p>For 'eu-region' filtering, Set 'region_name' of deployment.<p><strong>Note:</strong> We automatically infer region_name for Vertex AI, Bedrock, and IBM WatsonxAI based on your litellm params. For Azure, set <code>litellm.enable_preview = True</code>.<p><a href=https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/d33e49411d6503cb634f9652873160cd534dec96/litellm/router.py#L2958 target=_blank <h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=caching-across-model-groups>Caching across model groups<a href=#caching-across-model-groups class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Caching across model groups" title="Direct link to Caching across model groups">​</a></h2>

<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=alerting->Alerting 🚨<a href=#alerting- class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Alerting 🚨" title="Direct link to Alerting 🚨">​</a></h2>
<p>Send alerts to slack / your webhook url for the following events</p>
<ul>
<li>LLM API Exceptions</li>
<li>Slow LLM Responses</li>
</ul>
<p>Get a slack webhook url from <a href=https://api.slack.com/messaging/webhooks target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer">https://api.slack.com/messaging/webhooks</a></p>
<h4 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=usage>Usage<a href=#usage class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Usage" title="Direct link to Usage">​</a></h4>
<p>Initialize an <code>AlertingConfig</code> and pass it to <code>litellm.Router</code>. The following code will trigger an alert because <code>api_key=bad-key</code> which is invalid</p>

<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=track-cost-for-azure-deployments>Track cost for Azure Deployments<a href=#track-cost-for-azure-deployments class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Track cost for Azure Deployments" title="Direct link to Track cost for Azure Deployments">​</a></h2>
<p><strong>Problem</strong>: Azure returns <code>gpt-4</code> in the response when <code>azure/gpt-4-1106-preview</code> is used. This leads to inaccurate cost tracking</p>
<p><strong>Solution</strong> ✅ :  Set <code>model_info["base_model"]</code> on your router init so litellm uses the correct model for calculating azure cost</p>
<p>Step 1. Router Setup</p>

<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=deploy-router>Deploy Router<a href=#deploy-router class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Deploy Router" title="Direct link to Deploy Router">​</a></h2>
<p>If you want a server to load balance across different LLM APIs, use our <a href=/docs/simple_proxy>LiteLLM Proxy Server</a></p>
<h2 class="anchor anchorWithStickyNavbar_LWe7" id=debugging-router>Debugging Router<a href=#debugging-router class=hash-link aria-label="Direct link to Debugging Router" title="Direct link to Debugging Router">​</a></h2>

<p>Set <code>Router(set_verbose=True)</code></p>
<div class="language-python codeBlockContainer_Ckt0 theme-code-block" style=--prism-color:#000000;--prism-background-color:#ffffff><div class=codeBlockContent_QJqH><pre tabindex=0 class="prism-code language-python codeBlock_bY9V thin-scrollbar" style=color:#000000;background-color:#ffffff><code class=codeBlockLines_e6Vv><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token keyword" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 255)">from</span><span class="token plain"> litellm </span><span class="token keyword" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 255)">import</span><span class="token plain"> Router</span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain" style=display:inline-block></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">router </span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token plain"> Router</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">(</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">    model_list</span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token plain">model_list</span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">,</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain">    set_verbose</span><span class="token operator" style="color:rgb(0, 0, 0)">=</span><span class="token boolean">True</span><span class="token plain"></span><br></span><span class=token-line style=color:#000000><span class="token plain"></span><span class="token punctuation" style="color:rgb(4, 81, 165)">)</span><br></span></code></pre></div></div>
